MCP test cleanup disposition ledger
Scope: all 30 *.test.ts under apps/daemon/src/mcp. Recorded before test edits.
Original declarations: 415; planned dispositions: KEEP 408, DELETE 4, REWRITE 3. The deleted ${host} template represents two runtime tests.
Sources: docs/orquester-mcp.md sections below; AGENTS.md runtime boundaries; packages/api public wire types; provider fixtures referenced by compatibility tests.
A title identifies the exact input/race/failure variant, not merely a file-level KEEP. Each record applies all six bar items explicitly.
Shared fixtures are input data; FakeDaemonApi substitutes transport only. Assertions on method/path/body are public daemon protocol serialization, not private collaborator shape. Byte, row and cursor bounds below are transport/data-loss contracts, not visual geometry.
The server tools/list case is a literal network protocol contract, not a source/export inventory: strict schema and mutation hints affect callers. Its duplicate per-module constant comparison is deleted.
No production behavior changes planned. Risk: low for deletions/rewrites because specified stronger owners remain; security/storage/protocol cases kept. No whole helper/export loses its final caller.
Validation command from apps/daemon: pnpm exec node --import tsx --import ../../scripts/test/assert-ok.mjs --import ../../scripts/test/quiet-mock-timers.mjs --test --test-concurrency=2 src/mcp/*.test.ts src/mcp/tools/*.test.ts

Integration registrations (it callbacks): per-test dispositions
These cases were read with their production owners during the audit; the initial ledger enumeration matched test(...) only. They complete the 415-declaration inventory. None is E2E: each exercises the real host/store/index in a temporary in-process test host through the public DaemonApi wire boundary.

KEEP apps/daemon/src/mcp/history.integration.test.ts :: paging back by olderTurns through the tool never skips a turn, however much each read sheds
  Failure detected: a caller following olderTurns + 1 skips a turn or loops when read_transcript sheds the beginning of each page.
  B1 independent contract: docs/orquester-mcp.md §6 Messages says the returned olderTurns permits backward pagination without skipping turns, including turns omitted to fit maxChars.
  B2 caller failure: the loop follows the tool's actual cursor through 20 seeded turns; missing coverage, a non-decreasing cursor, or a falsely unavailable range fails the assertions.
  B3 oracle: the independently seeded turn numbers 1 through 20 and literal 15000-byte read budget define the expected coverage; no transcript/history function computes that expected set.
  B4 stable seam: read_transcript ToolDef.run over JSON-round-tripped DaemonApi responses backed by real orchestrator readThread/readHistory and a temporary SQLite index.
  B5 refactor stability: checks public cursor/range semantics and all-turn coverage, not SQL shape, internal helper calls, row markup or a fixed number of truncated pages.
  B6 lowest owner: neither history.test.ts's handcrafted pages nor transcript.test.ts's isolated shedding proves that real index cursors, history merging and tool-level shedding compose without lost turns. This one integration owns that interaction.
  Non-test callers: tools/messages.ts calls history.ts and transcript.ts; server.ts exposes messageTools; host orchestrator serves the same history interface. No seam removed.
  Remaining stronger coverage: none for this combination of real index pagination and repeated result shedding; unit cases retain malformed-page and budget-specific branches.
  Risk: user-visible transcript data loss or non-terminating pagination. Validation: root repository daemon test gate includes this file; no source change requires another focused run.

KEEP apps/daemon/src/mcp/history.integration.test.ts :: a turn larger than five pages: its latest rows are returned, and the hint names the call for the turns before it
  Failure detected: an oversized old turn consumes the bounded history walk, incorrectly reports completeness, loses its newest readable rows, or supplies a hint that cannot reach earlier turns.
  B1 independent contract: docs/orquester-mcp.md §6 Messages specifies bounded older-history reads, unavailableTurns/page-limit reporting and actionable beforeTurn/turns continuation; the public host history protocol pages activity blocks.
  B2 caller failure: a 2600-row turn must return the most recent 2000 activity rows through five real history pages, report the missing span, and the hinted follow-up must read earlier turns completely. The adjacent turn must also be reachable without spending every page inside the current retained turn.
  B3 oracle: seeded durable-log item identities, the literal five-page bound, literal 2000-row suffix and literal expected spans/hint parameters; logRowsOf reads input events, not production history results.
  B4 stable seam: readOlderHistory calls public DaemonApi history routes backed by the real orchestrator/index; unavailableHint supplies the externally presented continuation.
  B5 refactor stability: page count is the documented bounded-I/O protocol contract, and row count describes actual retained data; assertions do not inspect index implementation or private planner call shape.
  B6 lowest owner: handcrafted page-limit tests cannot catch disagreement with the host's real beforeSeq cursor minting or soft turn caps; this integration owns that cross-boundary bounded walk.
  Non-test callers: tools/messages.ts calls readOlderHistory/unavailableHint; host orchestrator serves readHistory. No helper becomes dead.
  Remaining stronger coverage: none for real oversized-turn block planning plus following the emitted hint; unit cases cover malformed/repeated cursors separately.
  Risk: inaccessible older conversations and misleading incomplete results. Validation: root repository daemon test gate includes this file.

KEEP apps/daemon/src/mcp/history.integration.test.ts :: restarted onto a fresh index: the turns the window no longer holds are named until the catch-up, then read whole
  Failure detected: rebuilding the disposable index silently claims evicted log history is complete, or catch-up fails to restore readability from the durable record.
  B1 independent contract: AGENTS.md makes events.ndjson the durable record and indexes disposable; docs/orquester-mcp.md §6 Messages requires unavailable spans while an index catches up, then normal readable history.
  B2 caller failure: restarting the host with the same seeded store and an empty real index must name missing turns without inventing pages; after real catchUp, those turns must be recovered completely in a public history read.
  B3 oracle: logRowsOf/missingOf compare results to independently seeded event identities; literal missing spans and empty-index wire fields are fixed expectations, not calculated by the code under test.
  B4 stable seam: host restart/store reuse, public snapshot/history reads, and the production index's catchUp API against a real temporary SQLite database.
  B5 refactor stability: assertions observe persistence/rebuild outcomes and public history bounds, not table layouts, cache formats, helper names or source text.
  B6 lowest owner: a history unit with supplied bounds cannot prove a real rebuilt index emits those bounds and later retrieves the same durable rows. Index-only tests cannot observe MCP's missing-turn reporting.
  Non-test callers: tools/messages.ts reads older history; agent-host startup/rebuild drives the same index and orchestrator. No test-only seam removed.
  Remaining stronger coverage: none for the complete restart -> honest missing span -> caught-up MCP read transition.
  Risk: durable conversation data appears lost after restart or index schema rebuild. Validation: root repository daemon test gate includes this file.

KEEP apps/daemon/src/mcp/tools/output.integration.test.ts :: a running shell grows between two pages: the next page continues at the last one's end, until it finishes
  Failure detected: a transcript's outputItemId cannot load its command, an output cache stops extending while the process runs, resumed paging duplicates/skips appended bytes, or running stays true after completion.
  B1 independent contract: docs/orquester-mcp.md §6 Messages and Tool output specify usable outputItemId links, byte offsets, Unicode-safe paging and running/completion flags for live command output.
  B2 caller failure: the transcript-provided shell reference is read through read_tool_output; after two real appended output chunks, the next page must contain exactly their text. Narrow pages reassemble the four-byte emoji, and a completion clears running without adding bytes.
  B3 oracle: CHUNKS and the literal later output string are independent input events; Buffer.byteLength computes expected UTF-8 offsets from those inputs rather than a production output helper.
  B4 stable seam: read_transcript -> read_tool_output ToolDef.run/schema via JSON-round-tripped DaemonApi, backed by real ingestion, fold and durable output reads.
  B5 refactor stability: checks public text/offset/flag behavior across appended events; no private cache shape, provider collaborator calls or source inspection.
  B6 lowest owner: isolated output tests substitute host replies and cannot catch real fold/log/output-link disagreement. This integration is stronger coverage for the deleted canned completed-shell passthrough cases, and uniquely owns growth between reads.
  Non-test callers: server.ts registers messageTools/outputTools; the host serves readThread/readItem/readToolOutputWindow. Shared shell helpers remain required.
  Remaining stronger coverage: none for this live read/append/read/complete sequence; unit tests retain older-host fallback and malformed-window edge cases.
  Risk: command output loss, broken drill-in links or falsely running commands. Validation: root repository daemon test gate includes this file.

KEEP apps/daemon/src/mcp/tools/output.integration.test.ts :: past the cap a running command loses its start — sixteen later calls of its agent, all more recently active, hold the slots — so its latest chunk is the entry and the id, and its whole output reads back from the log
  Failure detected: retention evicts a still-running command's start and MCP can no longer discover/read its complete output, even though the durable log still holds it.
  B1 independent contract: AGENTS.md's durable-event-record rule and docs/orquester-mcp.md §6 Messages/Tool output require a retained chunk to provide a usable output link when the opening row is absent, with full output loaded from the log.
  B2 caller failure: enough real parent/subagent activity is ingested to evict the server start; the test verifies that precondition, then requires the chunk-derived transcript entry and exact complete output, including chunks dropped from the retained window.
  B3 oracle: the served() input helper accumulates the actual strings ingested; expected output is their concatenation and expected link is the literal final seeded chunk id. No fold/transcript/output result generates those expectations.
  B4 stable seam: actual ingestion/retention plus public read_transcript/read_tool_output calls crossing DaemonApi's JSON wire boundary.
  B5 refactor stability: retention is exercised to reach a credible data-loss state, then only its observable absence and full-output recovery are asserted; there is no private retained-array layout or call-shape snapshot.
  B6 lowest owner: a handcrafted missing-start snapshot does not prove the real retention process leaves a usable chunk and that its id resolves against the durable log. This integration alone covers that complete path.
  Non-test callers: server.ts registers messageTools/outputTools; agent-host orchestrator owns ingestion and output reads. Helpers remain live for this integration.
  Remaining stronger coverage: none for actual cap eviction followed by discovery and full durable read; related transcript unit cases cover formatting/range selection only.
  Risk: long-running subagent commands become invisible or unreadable after unrelated work ages their start out. Validation: root repository daemon test gate includes this file.
